  + \frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{2}
  = \delta_{ij}\frac{\tau_i^2}{2} + \frac{\sigma_0^4}{2(p-q)},
\end{equation*}
which is \eqref{eq:Ginv_coupled}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem~\ref{thm:MLE_asymptotics} in block coordinates]\label{thm:MLE_CLT_coordinates}
Let $1 \leqslant q<p$ and $\theta_0 = [\bm{W}_0,\sigma_0^2] \in M$.
Define, on the event $\mathcal{A}_n$ of \eqref{eq:e_n_def}, the aligned
error blocks

\begin{equation}\label{eq:error_blocks}
  \begin{gathered}
  \bm{B}_n := \bm{U}_0^T
  \bracket{\hat{\bm{W}}_n\bm{Q}_n - \bm{W}_0}\bm{V}_0,
  \qquad
  \bm{C}_n := \bracket{\bm{U}_0^{\perp}}^T
  \bracket{\hat{\bm{W}}_n\bm{Q}_n - \bm{W}_0}\bm{V}_0,
  \\
  \bm{\Psi}_n := \frac{1}{2}\bracket{\bm{B}_n\bm{D}_0+\bm{D}_0\bm{B}_n^T},
  \qquad
  b_n := \hat{\sigma}_n^2 - \sigma_0^2,
  \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
with $\bm{Q}_n$ as in \eqref{eq:aligned_error}, and set
$(\bm{\Psi}_n,\bm{C}_n,b_n) := (\bm{0},\bm{0},0)$ off $\mathcal{A}_n$.
Then

\begin{equation}\label{eq:CLT_coordinates}
  \sqrt{n}\bracket{\bm{\Psi}_n,\ \bm{C}_n,\ b_n}
  \rightsquigarrow N\bracket{\bm{0},\ \mathcal{G}^{-1}}
  \qquad \text{in }
  \text{Sym}(q)\times\mathbb{R}^{(p-q)\times q}\times\mathbb{R},
\end{equation}
with $\mathcal{G}^{-1}$ given by
Proposition~\ref{thm:G_inverse_explicit}.
Explicitly, writing
$\bm{Z} = \bracket{\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}},\bm{Z}_{\bm{C}},Z_b}$ for the limit:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item the $(p-q)q$ entries of $\bm{Z}_{\bm{C}}$ are mutually
  independent, independent of $(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}},Z_b)$, and
  $\text{Var}\bracket{(\bm{Z}_{\bm{C}})_{ki}}
  = \frac{\sigma_0^2\tau_i}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)^2}$;
  \item the $\frac{q(q-1)}{2}$ strictly upper triangular entries of
  $\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}}$ are mutually independent, independent of everything
  else, and
  $\text{Var}\bracket{(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{ij}} = \frac{\tau_i\tau_j}{4}$
  for $i<j$;
  \item the remaining $q+1$ coordinates
  $\bracket{(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{11},\ldots,(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{qq},Z_b}$
  are jointly Gaussian with the covariances \eqref{eq:Ginv_coupled}; in
  particular
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:sigma2_CLT}
    \sqrt{n}\bracket{\hat{\sigma}_n^2 - \sigma_0^2}
    \rightsquigarrow N\bracket{0,\ \frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}} .
  \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \eqref{eq:aligned_error} we have
$\iota(\hat{\bm{u}}_n)
= \bracket{\hat{\bm{W}}_n\bm{Q}_n-\bm{W}_0,\ \hat{\sigma}_n^2-\sigma_0^2}$
on $\mathcal{A}_n$, so by \eqref{eq:B_C_Psi_def} the triple
$(\bm{\Psi}_n,\bm{C}_n,b_n)$ of \eqref{eq:error_blocks} is exactly
$\kappa(\hat{\bm{u}}_n)$ there; and both vanish off $\mathcal{A}_n$
because $\hat{\bm{u}}_n = \bm{0}$ there by \eqref{eq:e_n_def}.
Hence $(\bm{\Psi}_n,\bm{C}_n,b_n) = \kappa(\hat{\bm{u}}_n)$ identically.

Let $\bm{K}$ denote the matrix of $\kappa$ with respect to the
$g_{\theta_0}$-orthonormal basis $\bm{e}_1,\ldots,\bm{e}_d$ of
\eqref{eq:Delta_n_def} on the source and the scalar coordinates
\eqref{eq:scalar_coords} on the target.
Comparing \eqref{eq:Gcal_def} with
$\bm{G}_{jk} = G_{\theta_0}(\bm{e}_j,\bm{e}_k)$ gives
$\bm{G} = \bm{K}^T\mathcal{G}\bm{K}$, so

\begin{equation}\label{eq:pushforward}
  \bm{K}\bm{G}^{-1}\bm{K}^T
  = \bm{K}\bracket{\bm{K}^T\mathcal{G}\bm{K}}^{-1}\bm{K}^T
  = \bm{K}\bm{K}^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{-1}\bm{K}^{-T}\bm{K}^T
  = \mathcal{G}^{-1} .
\end{equation}
By Proposition~\ref{thm:MLE_asymptotics} we have
$\sqrt{n}\hat{\bm{u}}_n \rightsquigarrow N(\bm{0},\bm{G}^{-1})$ in the
coordinates of that basis, so the continuous mapping theorem applied to
the linear map $\bm{K}$ and the identity \eqref{eq:pushforward} give
\eqref{eq:CLT_coordinates}.

The three assertions are then read off
Proposition~\ref{thm:G_inverse_explicit}: a Gaussian vector whose
covariance matrix is block diagonal has independent blocks, and within the
first two groups the blocks are diagonal, so those coordinates are
mutually independent.
Statement \eqref{eq:sigma2_CLT} is the $(b,b)$ entry of
\eqref{eq:Ginv_coupled} together with
$b_n = \hat{\sigma}_n^2-\sigma_0^2$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{The score and the influence function in coordinates}

The same block decomposition makes the score \eqref{eq:score} and the
influence operator \eqref{eq:influence_function} completely explicit.
Throughout, for $\bm{x} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ we write

\begin{equation}\label{eq:y_z_def}
  \bm{y} := \bm{U}_0^T\bm{x} \in \mathbb{R}^q,
  \qquad
  \bm{z} := \bracket{\bm{U}_0^{\perp}}^T\bm{x} \in \mathbb{R}^{p-q},
\end{equation}
so that, by part 1 of Lemma~\ref{thm:block_form}, when
$\bm{X} \sim N_p(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_0)$ the vectors
$\bm{U}_0^T\bm{X}$ and $(\bm{U}_0^{\perp})^T\bm{X}$ are independent, the
first being $N_q(\bm{0},\bm{T}_0)$ and the second
$N_{p-q}(\bm{0},\sigma_0^2\bm{I}_{p-q})$.

\begin{proposition}[Score and influence function in coordinates]\label{thm:score_coords}
Let $\tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{x}) \in \mathbb{R}^d$ be the coordinate vector of
the score in the scalar coordinates \eqref{eq:scalar_coords}, defined by
$s(\bm{x};\theta_0)(\bm{h}) = \tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{x})^T\kappa(\bm{h})$ for
all $\bm{h} \in T_{\theta_0}M$; it is related to the vector $\bm{s}$ of
\eqref{eq:Delta_n_def} by $\bm{s} = \bm{K}^T\tilde{\bm{s}}$.
Then

\begin{equation}\label{eq:score_coords}
  \tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ii}} = \frac{\bm{y}_i^2-\tau_i}{\tau_i^2},
  \qquad
  \tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ij}}
  = \frac{2\bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j}{\tau_i\tau_j} \quad (i<j),
  \qquad
  \tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{C}_{ki}}
  = \frac{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)\,\bm{y}_i\bm{z}_k}{\sigma_0^2\tau_i},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:score_b}
  \tilde{\bm{s}}_b = \frac{1}{2}\squared{
  \sum_{i=1}^q\frac{\bm{y}_i^2-\tau_i}{\tau_i^2}
  + \frac{\|\bm{z}\|^2-(p-q)\sigma_0^2}{\sigma_0^4}} .
\end{equation}
Moreover $\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{X})} = \bm{0}$ and
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{X})\tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{X})^T}
= \mathcal{G}$, the influence operator \eqref{eq:influence_function} has coordinate
vector
$\kappa\bracket{\text{IF}(\bm{x})} = \mathcal{G}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{x})$,
and explicitly

\begin{equation}\label{eq:IF_coords}
  \text{IF}_b(\bm{x})
  = \frac{\bm{x}^T\bracket{\bm{I}_p-\bm{P}_0}\bm{x} - (p-q)\sigma_0^2}{p-q},
  \qquad
  \text{IF}_{\bm{C}_{ki}}(\bm{x})
  = \frac{\bm{y}_i\bm{z}_k}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:IF_Psi}
  \text{IF}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ij}}(\bm{x}) = \frac{\bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j}{2}
  \quad (i<j),
  \qquad
  \text{IF}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ii}}(\bm{x})
  = \frac{1}{2}\squared{\bm{y}_i^2 - \tau_i - \text{IF}_b(\bm{x})} .
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The relation $\bm{s} = \bm{K}^T\tilde{\bm{s}}$ is immediate from
$\bm{s}(\bm{x})^T\bm{h} = s(\bm{x};\theta_0)(\bm{h})
= \tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{x})^T\bm{K}\bm{h}$, valid for all $\bm{h}$.

Abbreviate $\bm{E} := \bm{\Sigma}_0^{-1}
\squared{\bm{x}\bm{x}^T-\bm{\Sigma}_0}\bm{\Sigma}_0^{-1}$, so that
\eqref{eq:score} reads
$s(\bm{x};\theta_0)(\bm{h})
= \frac{1}{2}\tr\curly{\bm{E}\,\mathcal{D}_0(\bm{h})}$.
Since $\bm{J}_0^T\bm{x} = \bracket{\bm{y}^T,\bm{z}^T}^T$ by
\eqref{eq:y_z_def}, part 1 of Lemma~\ref{thm:block_form} gives

\begin{equation}\label{eq:E_blocks}
  \bm{J}_0^T\bm{E}\bm{J}_0
  = \begin{pmatrix}
    \bm{T}_0^{-1}\bracket{\bm{y}\bm{y}^T-\bm{T}_0}\bm{T}_0^{-1}
    & \sigma_0^{-2}\bm{T}_0^{-1}\bm{y}\bm{z}^T \\
    \sigma_0^{-2}\bm{z}\bm{y}^T\bm{T}_0^{-1}
    & \sigma_0^{-4}\bracket{\bm{z}\bm{z}^T-\sigma_0^2\bm{I}_{p-q}}
  \end{pmatrix}
  =: \begin{pmatrix}\bm{E}_{11} & \bm{E}_{12}\\
  \bm{E}_{12}^T & \bm{E}_{22}\end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
Pairing \eqref{eq:E_blocks} with \eqref{eq:Dh_blocks}, the two off-diagonal
contributions to the trace are equal, because
$\tr\bracket{\bm{E}_{12}^T\bm{D}_0\bm{C}^T}
= \tr\bracket{\bracket{\bm{E}_{12}^T\bm{D}_0\bm{C}^T}^T}
= \tr\bracket{\bm{C}\bm{D}_0\bm{E}_{12}}
= \tr\bracket{\bm{E}_{12}\bm{C}\bm{D}_0}$, and therefore

\begin{equation}\label{eq:score_expanded}
  2\,s(\bm{x};\theta_0)(\bm{h})
  = \tr\bracket{\bm{E}_{11}\squared{2\bm{\Psi}+b_{\bm{h}}\bm{I}_q}}
  + \frac{2}{\sigma_0^2}\,\bm{z}^T\bm{C}\bm{D}_0\bm{T}_0^{-1}\bm{y}
  + b_{\bm{h}}\tr\bm{E}_{22} .
\end{equation}
Now read off the coefficient of each scalar coordinate.
The matrix $\bm{\Psi}$ is symmetric, so
$\tr\bracket{\bm{E}_{11}\bm{\Psi}}
= \sum_i (\bm{E}_{11})_{ii}\bm{\Psi}_{ii}
+ 2\sum_{i<j}(\bm{E}_{11})_{ij}\bm{\Psi}_{ij}$, while
$(\bm{E}_{11})_{ii} = \frac{\bm{y}_i^2-\tau_i}{\tau_i^2}$ and
$(\bm{E}_{11})_{ij} = \frac{\bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j}{\tau_i\tau_j}$ for
$i \neq j$; dividing the resulting $2\tr(\bm{E}_{11}\bm{\Psi})$ by the
factor $2$ in \eqref{eq:score_expanded} gives the first two formulas of
\eqref{eq:score_coords}.
The second term of \eqref{eq:score_expanded} equals
$\frac{2}{\sigma_0^2}\sum_{k,i}\bm{C}_{ki}\bm{z}_k
\frac{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)\bm{y}_i}{\tau_i}$, which after division by $2$
gives the third formula of \eqref{eq:score_coords}.
The coefficient of $b_{\bm{h}}$ is
$\frac{1}{2}\squared{\tr\bm{E}_{11}+\tr\bm{E}_{22}}$, which is
\eqref{eq:score_b}.

The identities $\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}} = \bm{0}$ and
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}\tilde{\bm{s}}^T} = \mathcal{G}$ are
Theorem~\ref{thm:DQM} read through \eqref{eq:Gcal_def}, since for all
$\bm{h}_1,\bm{h}_2 \in T_{\theta_0}M$

\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{E}\squared{s(\bm{X};\theta_0)(\bm{h}_1)\,
  s(\bm{X};\theta_0)(\bm{h}_2)}
  = G_{\theta_0}(\bm{h}_1,\bm{h}_2)
  = \kappa(\bm{h}_1)^T\mathcal{G}\kappa(\bm{h}_2)
\end{equation*}
and $\kappa$ is onto.
They can also be checked directly from \eqref{eq:score_coords}: with
$\bm{y}$ and $\bm{z}$ independent centred Gaussians,
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ii}}^2}
= \text{Var}\bracket{\bm{y}_i^2}/\tau_i^4 = 2/\tau_i^2$,
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ij}}^2}
= 4/(\tau_i\tau_j)$,
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{C}_{ki}}^2}
= \sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)^2\tau_i\sigma_0^2/\bracket{\sigma_0^4\tau_i^2}
= \sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)^2/\bracket{\sigma_0^2\tau_i}$,
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ii}}\tilde{\bm{s}}_b}
= 1/\tau_i^2$ and
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\tilde{\bm{s}}_b^2} = \eta$, every product containing
an odd power of a coordinate of $\bm{z}$ having mean zero; these are
exactly the entries \eqref{eq:Gcal_blocks}.

By Definition~\ref{def:G_inverse} the vector $\text{IF}(\bm{x})$ of
\eqref{eq:influence_function} is characterized by
$G_{\theta_0}\bracket{\text{IF}(\bm{x}),\bm{h}}
= s(\bm{x};\theta_0)(\bm{h})$ for all $\bm{h}$, that is, by
\eqref{eq:Gcal_def}, by
$\kappa\bracket{\text{IF}(\bm{x})}^T\mathcal{G}\kappa(\bm{h})
= \tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{x})^T\kappa(\bm{h})$ for all $\bm{h}$; since $\kappa$
is onto, $\mathcal{G}\kappa\bracket{\text{IF}(\bm{x})}
= \tilde{\bm{s}}(\bm{x})$.

It remains to compute $\mathcal{G}^{-1}\tilde{\bm{s}}$ block by block.
For $i<j$, by \eqref{eq:Ginv_diagonal},
$\text{IF}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ij}}
= \frac{\tau_i\tau_j}{4}\cdot\frac{2\bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j}{\tau_i\tau_j}
= \frac{\bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j}{2}$, and
$\text{IF}_{\bm{C}_{ki}}
= \frac{\sigma_0^2\tau_i}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)^2}
\cdot\frac{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)\bm{y}_i\bm{z}_k}{\sigma_0^2\tau_i}
= \frac{\bm{y}_i\bm{z}_k}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)}$, which are the second
formula of \eqref{eq:IF_coords} and the first of \eqref{eq:IF_Psi}.
On the coupled block, \eqref{eq:Ginv_coupled} and \eqref{eq:score_b} give

\begin{equation*}
  \begin{split}
  \text{IF}_b
  & = \frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}\,\tilde{\bm{s}}_b
  - \frac{\sigma_0^4}{p-q}\sum_{i=1}^q \tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ii}}
  \\ & = \frac{\sigma_0^4}{p-q}\sum_{i=1}^q
  \frac{\bm{y}_i^2-\tau_i}{\tau_i^2}
  + \frac{\|\bm{z}\|^2-(p-q)\sigma_0^2}{p-q}
  - \frac{\sigma_0^4}{p-q}\sum_{i=1}^q\frac{\bm{y}_i^2-\tau_i}{\tau_i^2}
  = \frac{\|\bm{z}\|^2-(p-q)\sigma_0^2}{p-q},
  \end{split}
\end{equation*}
the $\bm{y}$ terms cancelling identically; since
$\|\bm{z}\|^2 = \bm{x}^T\bm{U}_0^{\perp}(\bm{U}_0^{\perp})^T\bm{x}
= \bm{x}^T\bracket{\bm{I}_p-\bm{P}_0}\bm{x}$ by \eqref{eq:J_0_def}, this is
the first formula of \eqref{eq:IF_coords}.
Similarly

\begin{equation*}
  \begin{split}
  \text{IF}_{\bm{\Psi}_{ii}}
  & = \sum_{j=1}^q\squared{\delta_{ij}\frac{\tau_i^2}{2}
  + \frac{\sigma_0^4}{2(p-q)}}\tilde{\bm{s}}_{\bm{\Psi}_{jj}}
  - \frac{\sigma_0^4}{p-q}\,\tilde{\bm{s}}_b
  \\ & = \frac{\bm{y}_i^2-\tau_i}{2}
  + \frac{\sigma_0^4}{2(p-q)}\sum_{j=1}^q\frac{\bm{y}_j^2-\tau_j}{\tau_j^2}
  - \frac{\sigma_0^4}{2(p-q)}\sum_{j=1}^q\frac{\bm{y}_j^2-\tau_j}{\tau_j^2}
  - \frac{\|\bm{z}\|^2-(p-q)\sigma_0^2}{2(p-q)},
  \end{split}
\end{equation*}
which is the second formula of \eqref{eq:IF_Psi}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The noise variance]\label{thm:sigma2_summary}
The maximum likelihood estimator of the noise variance satisfies
\eqref{eq:sigma2_CLT}, and it is asymptotically linear with influence
function

\begin{equation}\label{eq:IF_sigma2}
  \text{IF}_{\sigma^2}(\bm{x})
  = \frac{\bm{x}^T\bracket{\bm{I}_p-\bm{P}_0}\bm{x} - (p-q)\sigma_0^2}{p-q},
  \qquad
  \mathbb{E}\squared{\text{IF}_{\sigma^2}(\bm{X})} = 0,
  \qquad
  \mathbb{E}\squared{\text{IF}_{\sigma^2}(\bm{X})^2}
  = \frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The weak limit is \eqref{eq:sigma2_CLT}.
The influence function is the first formula of \eqref{eq:IF_coords},
because $b_n = \hat{\sigma}_n^2-\sigma_0^2$ is the $b$ coordinate of
$\kappa(\hat{\bm{u}}_n)$ by \eqref{eq:error_blocks}, so that the $b$
coordinate of \eqref{eq:asc_linearity} reads
$\sqrt{n}\bracket{\hat{\sigma}_n^2-\sigma_0^2}
= n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^n \text{IF}_b(\bm{X}_i)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)$.
For the moments, $\bracket{\bm{U}_0^{\perp}}^T\bm{X}
\sim N_{p-q}\bracket{\bm{0},\sigma_0^2\bm{I}_{p-q}}$ gives
$\mathbb{E}\|\bm{z}\|^2 = (p-q)\sigma_0^2$ and
$\text{Var}\bracket{\|\bm{z}\|^2} = 2(p-q)\sigma_0^4$, whence
$\mathbb{E}\squared{\text{IF}_{\sigma^2}^2}
= \frac{2(p-q)\sigma_0^4}{(p-q)^2} = \frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}$, consistent
with \eqref{eq:sigma2_CLT} as it must be, since the asymptotic covariance
of an asymptotically linear sequence is the covariance of its influence
function.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Where the model helps, and where it does not]\label{rmk:IF_vs_S_n}
Formulas \eqref{eq:IF_coords}--\eqref{eq:IF_Psi} answer a question the
asymptotic theory alone does not: in which directions is the constrained
estimator $\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_n$ actually better than the sample covariance
matrix $\bm{S}_n$?
The answer is: in exactly one block, and the reason is the computation of
$\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_n$ carried out in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:consistency}, which exhibits it as $\bm{S}_n$ with the
trailing $p-q$ eigenvalues replaced by their average.

By \eqref{eq:Sigma_linear} the influence function of
$\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_n$ is $\mathcal{D}_0 \circ \text{IF}$, whose blocks
are, by \eqref{eq:Dh_blocks},
$2\text{IF}_{\bm{\Psi}}+\text{IF}_b\bm{I}_q$,
$\text{IF}_{\bm{C}}\bm{D}_0$ and $\text{IF}_b\bm{I}_{p-q}$.
By \eqref{eq:IF_coords}--\eqref{eq:IF_Psi} these are

\begin{equation}\label{eq:IF_Sigma_blocks}
  \bracket{2\text{IF}_{\bm{\Psi}}+\text{IF}_b\bm{I}_q}_{ij}
  = \bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j - \delta_{ij}\tau_i,
  \qquad
  \bracket{\text{IF}_{\bm{C}}\bm{D}_0}_{ki}
  = \sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)\frac{\bm{y}_i\bm{z}_k}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)}
  = \bm{z}_k\bm{y}_i,
  \qquad
  \text{IF}_b\bm{I}_{p-q},
\end{equation}
the diagonal case of the first identity being exactly the cancellation of
$\text{IF}_b$ in the second formula of \eqref{eq:IF_Psi}.
Now the influence function of $\bm{S}_n$, read in the same basis
\eqref{eq:J_0_def}, is
$\bm{J}_0^T\bracket{\bm{x}\bm{x}^T-\bm{\Sigma}_0}\bm{J}_0$, whose blocks
are $\bm{y}\bm{y}^T-\bm{T}_0$, $\bm{z}\bm{y}^T$ and
$\bm{z}\bm{z}^T-\sigma_0^2\bm{I}_{p-q}$.
The first two coincide with \eqref{eq:IF_Sigma_blocks}: \textit{to first
order the maximum likelihood fit reproduces the sample covariance matrix
in the signal block and in the signal--noise block, and gains nothing
there.}
Only the trailing block differs, where
$\bm{z}\bm{z}^T-\sigma_0^2\bm{I}_{p-q}$ is replaced by its average

\begin{equation*}
  \frac{\tr\bracket{\bm{z}\bm{z}^T-\sigma_0^2\bm{I}_{p-q}}}{p-q}
  \bm{I}_{p-q} = \text{IF}_b\,\bm{I}_{p-q} :
\end{equation*}
the isotropy constraint of the model averages the $p-q$ noise directions,
dividing the variance by $p-q$.
This is the infinitesimal shadow of the exact identity established in the
proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:consistency}, which says the same thing
non-asymptotically: $\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_n$ differs from $\bm{S}_n$ only
through the trailing eigenvalues, which it replaces by their mean.
It also explains why the leading blocks cannot be improved: by
\eqref{eq:D_0_image} the model imposes no constraint there at all, the
image of $\mathcal{D}_0$ being everything except for the isotropy of the
trailing block.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Cross-checks against classical principal component
analysis]\label{rmk:pca_crosschecks}
Three consistency checks, each of which recovers a classical formula, and
none of which is used elsewhere.

\textbf{1. Pooled noise eigenvalues.}
The variance $\frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}$ of \eqref{eq:sigma2_CLT} is the
classical asymptotic variance of the average of the $p-q$ sample
eigenvalues attached to a population eigenvalue of multiplicity $p-q$.
Indeed $\hat{\sigma}_n^2 = \frac{1}{p-q}\sum_{j>q}\lambda_j(\bm{S}_n)$
exactly, by \eqref{eq:sigma_hat_def}, and

\begin{equation*}
  \sum_{j>q}\lambda_j(\bm{S}_n)
  = \tr(\bm{S}_n) - \max\curly{\tr\bracket{\bm{P}\bm{S}_n}
  : \bm{P} \text{ an orthogonal projection of rank } q}
\end{equation*}
by Ky Fan's variational principle.
Write $\bm{P}_n$ for the maximizer, which is the projection onto the span
of $\bm{v}_1,\ldots,\bm{v}_q$.
Since $\bm{P}_0$ is the maximizer at $\bm{\Sigma}_0$, by
\eqref{eq:eigengap}, we have on the one hand
$\tr(\bm{P}_n\bm{S}_n) \geqslant \tr(\bm{P}_0\bm{S}_n)$ and on the other

\begin{equation*}
  \tr\bracket{\bm{P}_n\bm{S}_n} - \tr\bracket{\bm{P}_0\bm{S}_n}
  \leqslant \tr\bracket{\bracket{\bm{P}_n-\bm{P}_0}
  \bracket{\bm{S}_n-\bm{\Sigma}_0}}
  \leqslant \|\bm{P}_n-\bm{P}_0\|_F\|\bm{S}_n-\bm{\Sigma}_0\|_F ,
\end{equation*}
because $\tr(\bm{P}_n\bm{\Sigma}_0) \leqslant \tr(\bm{P}_0\bm{\Sigma}_0)$.
The multivariate central limit theorem applied to the independent,
identically distributed and square integrable matrices
$\bm{X}_i\bm{X}_i^T$ gives
$\|\bm{S}_n-\bm{\Sigma}_0\|_F = O_{\mathbb{P}}\bracket{n^{-\frac{1}{2}}}$,
and the Davis--Kahan $\sin\Theta$ theorem, with the eigengap
\eqref{eq:eigengap}, gives
$\|\bm{P}_n-\bm{P}_0\|_F = O_{\mathbb{P}}\bracket{n^{-\frac{1}{2}}}$ as
well (see for example \citealp{Chen_et_al_2021}, Chapter 2, or
\citealp{Kato_1995}, Chapter II).
Hence $\bm{P}_n$ may be replaced by $\bm{P}_0$ at a cost of
$O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})$, and the statistic that remains is
$\frac{1}{p-q}\tr\bracket{\bracket{\bm{I}_p-\bm{P}_0}\bm{S}_n}$, whose
centred and $\sqrt{n}$-scaled version is exactly the average of the
influence functions \eqref{eq:IF_sigma2}.

\textbf{2. The leading block is not improved.}
By \eqref{eq:Ginv_coupled}, and writing
$\text{Var}\bracket{2(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{ii}+Z_b}
= 4\text{Var}\bracket{(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{ii}}
+ 4\text{Cov}\bracket{(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{ii},Z_b}
+ \text{Var}(Z_b)$,

\begin{equation*}
  \text{Var}\bracket{2(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{ii}+Z_b}
  = 4\bracket{\frac{\tau_i^2}{2}+\frac{\sigma_0^4}{2(p-q)}}
  + 4\bracket{-\frac{\sigma_0^4}{p-q}} + \frac{2\sigma_0^4}{p-q}
  = 2\tau_i^2
  = \text{Var}\bracket{\bracket{\bm{U}_0^T\bm{X}}_i^2},
\end{equation*}
and likewise
$\text{Var}\bracket{2(\bm{Z}_{\bm{\Psi}})_{ij}} = \tau_i\tau_j
= \text{Var}\bracket{\bm{y}_i\bm{y}_j}$ for $i<j$ --- the sample
covariance variances for those coordinates, as
Remark~\ref{rmk:IF_vs_S_n} requires.

\textbf{3. Anderson's eigenvector variances.}
Suppose the $\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)$ are simple.
By part 2 of Lemma~\ref{thm:block_form}, the component of the aligned
loading error orthogonal to the column space of $\bm{W}_0$ is
$\bm{U}_0^{\perp}\bm{C}_n\bm{V}_0^T$; since the $i$-th column of
$\bm{W}_0\bm{V}_0$ is $\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)$ times the $i$-th column of
$\bm{U}_0$, the tilt of the estimated $i$-th direction out of the signal
subspace is $\bm{U}_0^{\perp}$ applied to the $i$-th column of
$\bm{C}_n$, divided by $\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)$, with limiting
per-coordinate variance

\begin{equation*}
  \frac{1}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)^2}\cdot
  \frac{\sigma_0^2\tau_i}{\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)^2}
  = \frac{\sigma_0^2\tau_i}{\bracket{\tau_i-\sigma_0^2}^2}
  = \frac{\lambda_i(\bm{\Sigma}_0)\lambda_j(\bm{\Sigma}_0)}
  {\bracket{\lambda_i(\bm{\Sigma}_0)-\lambda_j(\bm{\Sigma}_0)}^2}
  \qquad \text{for any } j>q,
\end{equation*}
which is Anderson's formula for the noise directions.
When the $\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)$ are repeated this
eigenvector-by-eigenvector reading breaks down --- individual eigenvectors
are then not asymptotically normal --- but nothing in
Propositions~\ref{thm:G_explicit} and \ref{thm:G_inverse_explicit} is
affected: no formula in this section divides by a difference of singular
values, and the only inverted quantities are $\tau_i$, $\sigma_0^2$,
$\sigma_i(\bm{W}_0)$ and $p-q$, all strictly positive under the standing
assumptions $\rank(\bm{W}_0) = q$, $\sigma_0^2>0$ and $q<p$.
This is the promised quantitative form of the third comment of
Remark~\ref{rmk:MLE_CLT_comments}.
\end{remark}
